### Text2Model: Generating dynamic chemical reactor models using large language models (LLMs)
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17004v1)
- **Authors**: Sophia Rupprecht, Yassine Hounat, Monisha Kumar, Giacomo Lastrucci, Artur M. Schweidtmann
- **Abstract**: As large language models have shown remarkable capabilities in conversing via natural language, the question arises as to how LLMs could potentially assist chemical engineers in research and industry with domain-specific tasks. We generate dynamic chemical reactor models in Modelica code format from textual descriptions as user input. We fine-tune Llama 3.1 8B Instruct on synthetically generated Modelica code for different reactor scenarios. We compare the performance of our fine-tuned model to the baseline Llama 3.1 8B Instruct model and GPT4o. We manually assess the models' predictions regarding the syntactic and semantic accuracy of the generated dynamic models. We find that considerable improvements are achieved by the fine-tuned model with respect to both the semantic and the syntactic accuracy of the Modelica models. However, the fine-tuned model lacks a satisfactory ability to generalize to unseen scenarios compared to GPT4o.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the application of large language models (LLMs) to generate dynamic chemical reactor models in Modelica code from natural language descriptions. The authors fine-tune a Llama 3.1 8B Instruct model on synthetically generated Modelica code representing various reactor scenarios.  They compare the performance of the fine-tuned model against the baseline Llama 3.1 8B Instruct model and GPT4o in terms of syntactic and semantic accuracy.  The results demonstrate that fine-tuning significantly improves performance in reproducing scenarios seen during training, but the fine-tuned model struggles to generalize to unseen scenarios compared to GPT4o. The authors identify areas for improvement, including expanding the training dataset, incorporating external knowledge through Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG), and implementing an iterative feedback loop with Dymola simulation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the application of LLMs to generate dynamic chemical reactor models in Modelica, which is a domain-specific language not widely addressed in LLM research. While existing works explore code generation using LLMs, focusing on Modelica and chemical engineering problems adds a valuable contribution. However, the core idea of fine-tuning an LLM for code generation isn't entirely new, thus limiting the overall novelty.
* **Significance:** The paper addresses a practical problem in chemical engineering: the time-consuming process of creating and maintaining complex dynamic models. Automating this process using LLMs could potentially reduce development time and improve model accuracy. The identified shortcomings, especially the generalization issue, provide valuable insights for future research in this area.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Well-defined problem:** The paper clearly defines a specific, practical problem within the chemical engineering domain.
    * **Comprehensive evaluation:** The authors use a comprehensive evaluation procedure with well-defined metrics for assessing syntactic and semantic accuracy, providing a detailed analysis of the models' performance.
    * **Clear results and discussion:** The results are clearly presented and discussed, highlighting the strengths and weaknesses of the fine-tuned model.
    * **Identified avenues for improvement:** The paper identifies specific areas for future research, such as expanding the training dataset and incorporating external knowledge.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited generalization:**  The most significant weakness is the poor generalization performance of the fine-tuned model. The fact that GPT4o, without fine-tuning on the domain-specific data, outperforms the fine-tuned model on unseen scenarios is a significant limitation. This undermines the practical value of the approach.  The training dataset is potentially not diverse enough.
    * **Synthetic data generation:** Relying solely on synthetically generated data raises questions about the model's ability to handle real-world scenarios with more complex and nuanced descriptions.
    * **Limited model comparison:** While comparing against the baseline model and GPT4o is useful, comparing against other code generation approaches or alternative fine-tuning strategies could provide a more comprehensive evaluation.
    * **Lack of real-world validation:** The generated models are evaluated primarily through manual inspection. Validating the models using real-world experimental data or industry case studies would strengthen the paper's findings.

* **Overall Impact:**  While the paper demonstrates the potential of LLMs for Modelica code generation, the limited generalization ability significantly restricts its immediate practical impact. However, the paper contributes valuable insights into the challenges and opportunities in applying LLMs to domain-specific languages, laying the groundwork for future research.  The detailed evaluation methodology and identified areas for improvement are particularly helpful. The paper would benefit from exploring more sophisticated fine-tuning techniques or architectures better suited for handling code generation in this domain.

**Score: 5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a well-defined problem and demonstrates that fine-tuning can improve LLM performance for Modelica code generation. However, the significant limitation of poor generalization, especially compared to GPT4o without domain-specific training, significantly dampens the paper's impact. The novelty is moderate, as the core idea is based on existing code generation practices. The contribution is helpful in identifying challenges and suggesting future directions, but the current state of the fine-tuned model offers limited practical utility. The score reflects the balance between the well-executed aspects of the study and the crucial shortcoming of limited generalization.

- **Classification**: cs.PL
- **Score**: 5/10

### DIDiffGes: Decoupled Semi-Implicit Diffusion Models for Real-time Gesture Generation from Speech
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17059v1)
- **Authors**: Yongkang Cheng, Shaoli Huang, Xuelin Chen, Jifeng Ning, Mingming Gong
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have demonstrated remarkable synthesis quality and diversity in generating co-speech gestures. However, the computationally intensive sampling steps associated with diffusion models hinder their practicality in real-world applications. Hence, we present DIDiffGes, for a Decoupled Semi-Implicit Diffusion model-based framework, that can synthesize high-quality, expressive gestures from speech using only a few sampling steps. Our approach leverages Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) to enable large-step sampling for diffusion model. We decouple gesture data into body and hands distributions and further decompose them into marginal and conditional distributions. GANs model the marginal distribution implicitly, while L2 reconstruction loss learns the conditional distributions exciplictly. This strategy enhances GAN training stability and ensures expressiveness of generated full-body gestures. Our framework also learns to denoise root noise conditioned on local body representation, guaranteeing stability and realism. DIDiffGes can generate gestures from speech with just 10 sampling steps, without compromising quality and expressiveness, reducing the number of sampling steps by a factor of 100 compared to existing methods. Our user study reveals that our method outperforms state-of-the-art approaches in human likeness, appropriateness, and style correctness. Project is https://cyk990422.github.io/DIDiffGes.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the paper and will provide a summary, a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance, and a score.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DIDiffGes, a new diffusion model-based framework for generating realistic co-speech gestures in real-time. The core innovation lies in decoupling the diffusion model's process into separate noise components for body and hand movements, trained using a semi-implicit approach combining adversarial learning (GANs) and L2 loss. This decoupling aims to capture the varying complexities of body and hand gestures better, leading to more expressive and accurate synthesis. The "semi-implicit" training allows for larger step sizes in the diffusion process, significantly reducing the required sampling steps and thus enabling real-time performance. The authors also employ a sequential denoiser strategy to recover root noise conditioned on local body representations, mitigating foot-sliding and body-drifting artifacts. The paper demonstrates through quantitative metrics (FGD, BA, DIV) and user studies that DIDiffGes achieves state-of-the-art performance regarding gesture quality, expressiveness, and real-time capability, outperforming existing diffusion and GAN-based methods while requiring significantly fewer sampling steps.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel ideas.
    *   The decoupling of body and hand noise components is a key contribution, allowing for specialized learning tailored to each part's movement dynamics. This has face validity, as hands and body have different characteristics.
    *   The semi-implicit training approach, combining adversarial learning and L2 loss, is crucial for enabling large-step sampling in the diffusion model.  While inspired by SIDDM, applying it to the gesture domain and decoupling the noise components is novel.
    *   The sequential denoiser that handles root joint motion after local motion noise reduction appears to offer improved realism.

*   **Significance:**  The potential impact of this work is high.
    *   Real-time co-speech gesture generation is a crucial element for creating more natural and engaging human-computer interactions. The speed bottleneck of diffusion models has limited their practical use. Overcoming this hurdle is a major contribution.
    *   The improved quality and expressiveness demonstrated by DIDiffGes could significantly enhance the realism and appeal of virtual avatars and conversational agents.
    *   The paper provides a solid foundation for future research in accelerating diffusion-based motion synthesis and developing more sophisticated control mechanisms for virtual characters.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly articulates the problem and the limitations of existing approaches.
    *   The method is well-motivated and explained in detail.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate significant improvements over state-of-the-art methods, both quantitatively and qualitatively. The user study provides valuable validation of the human-perceived improvements.
    *   The inclusion of ablation studies demonstrates the importance of each component of the proposed framework.
    *   The availability of the project page and an A2G repository for DIDiffGes allows for further exploration and adoption of the technology.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper outlines the technical details, some aspects (like the exact architecture of the GAN discriminators and the conditional control encoder) are deferred to the supplementary material, hindering a complete understanding of the implementation.
    *   Although the paper mentions using style labels, the control mechanism beyond using them as conditional features in the transformer needs more explanation. How well the model adapts to different styles would be beneficial to elaborate on.
    *   The paper provides a quantitative comparison of sampling speed (inference time), but could further explore the computational cost in terms of memory usage. This would be especially relevant in real-time applications with limited resources.
    *   There's a lack of discussion on potential failure modes of the model or limitations in generating gestures with extreme or complex characteristics.  What range of expressiveness does it really cover?

* **Concerns:** The writing quality can be improved for better clarity and conciseness.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the combination of novelty, significance, strong experimental results, and addressing the key challenge of real-time performance in diffusion-based gesture generation, I believe this paper represents a significant contribution to the field.  The decoupling approach and semi-implicit training are clever ideas that enable a substantial leap in efficiency while maintaining (or even improving) gesture quality.  While there are some minor weaknesses in the explanation and a few areas that could be further explored, the paper's overall impact is substantial. It pushes forward the capabilities of human-computer interaction significantly.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 8/10

### PVChat: Personalized Video Chat with One-Shot Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17069v1)
- **Authors**: Yufei Shi, Weilong Yan, Gang Xu, Yumeng Li, Yuchen Li, Zhenxi Li, Fei Richard Yu, Ming Li, Si Yong Yeo
- **Abstract**: Video large language models (ViLLMs) excel in general video understanding, e.g., recognizing activities like talking and eating, but struggle with identity-aware comprehension, such as "Wilson is receiving chemotherapy" or "Tom is discussing with Sarah", limiting their applicability in smart healthcare and smart home environments. To address this limitation, we propose a one-shot learning framework PVChat, the first personalized ViLLM that enables subject-aware question answering (QA) from a single video for each subject. Our approach optimizes a Mixture-of-Heads (MoH) enhanced ViLLM on a synthetically augmented video-QA dataset, leveraging a progressive image-to-video learning strategy. Specifically, we introduce an automated augmentation pipeline that synthesizes identity-preserving positive samples and retrieves hard negatives from existing video corpora, generating a diverse training dataset with four QA types: existence, appearance, action, and location inquiries. To enhance subject-specific learning, we propose a ReLU Routing MoH attention mechanism, alongside two novel objectives: (1) Smooth Proximity Regularization for progressive learning through exponential distance scaling and (2) Head Activation Enhancement for balanced attention routing. Finally, we adopt a two-stage training strategy, transitioning from image pre-training to video fine-tuning, enabling a gradual learning process from static attributes to dynamic representations. We evaluate PVChat on diverse datasets covering medical scenarios, TV series, anime, and real-world footage, demonstrating its superiority in personalized feature understanding after learning from a single video, compared to state-of-the-art ViLLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PVChat, a novel personalized Video Large Language Model (ViLLM) designed to enable subject-aware question answering from a single reference video.  The key idea is to address the limitations of existing ViLLMs that struggle with identity-aware comprehension (e.g., recognizing specific individuals and their actions in videos). PVChat uses a Mixture-of-Heads (MoH) enhanced ViLLM trained on a synthetically augmented video-QA dataset. The data augmentation pipeline generates identity-preserving positive samples and retrieves hard negatives to improve discrimination.  The MoH mechanism incorporates a ReLU Routing approach and two novel objectives: Smooth Proximity Regularization and Head Activation Enhancement. A two-stage training strategy (image pre-training followed by video fine-tuning) facilitates learning from static attributes to dynamic representations. The paper evaluates PVChat on various datasets, demonstrating superior performance in personalized feature understanding compared to state-of-the-art ViLLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper makes several noteworthy contributions:

*   **Problem Formulation:**  Identifying the limitations of existing ViLLMs regarding personalized video understanding is a valuable contribution. While personalization has been explored in image understanding, extending it to video and specifically focusing on identity-aware comprehension is novel.
*   **One-Shot Learning Framework:** The proposed PVChat framework, enabling subject-aware QA from a single video, is a significant advancement. This one-shot learning capability reduces the dependency on large amounts of training data specific to each individual, which would be difficult to collect in many real-world scenarios.
*   **Data Augmentation Pipeline:**  The automated pipeline for generating identity-preserving positive samples and hard negatives is a solid contribution. The combination of facial attribute extraction, video synthesis tools (ConsisID, PhotoMaker, LivePortrait), and hard negative mining from large-scale datasets (Laion-Face-5B, CelebV-HQ) is effective in creating a diverse and challenging training dataset.  The generation of QA pairs covering existence, appearance, action, and location adds to the value.
*   **ReMoH Mechanism:** The ReLU Routing Mixture-of-Heads (ReMoH) attention mechanism is technically sound. ReLU-driven routing allows for a smoother and more scalable training process compared to conventional softmax-based MoH implementations, and the smoothness Regularization and Head Activation Enhancement are also useful for this specific personalized learning task.

**Significance:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field:

*   **Practical Applications:**  The ability of PVChat to perform personalized video understanding unlocks numerous real-world applications in smart healthcare, smart homes, human-computer interaction, and personalized assistance. For example, it could be used to monitor a patient's activity in a hospital room and provide alerts based on their specific condition or medication schedule.
*   **Performance Gains:** The experimental results demonstrate the superiority of PVChat in personalized feature understanding compared to state-of-the-art ViLLMs, which is a strong indication of its effectiveness.
*   **Dataset and Code Release:** The public release of the diverse dataset and code will facilitate further research in this area.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Synthetic Data:** While the data augmentation pipeline is a strong point, the reliance on synthetic data can be a limitation. The generated videos might not fully capture the complexity and nuances of real-world scenarios, which could affect the generalizability of the model. It would strengthen the paper to include a small test set that is purely real videos as a comparison.
*   **Limited Evaluation of Generalization:** The evaluation focuses primarily on datasets covering medical scenarios, TV series, anime, and real-world footage. Further evaluation on diverse datasets or real-world applications would provide a more comprehensive assessment of the model's generalization ability.
*   **Complexity:** The proposed framework is relatively complex, involving several components (MoH, SPR, HAE). Simpler baseline ablation would be useful.
*   **The prompts in the figures need to be edited/revised to ensure that they provide better and less ambiguous context,** since many of them are quite vague.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **8**.

*   The paper addresses an important and timely problem, that many models perform well as generalists but perform poorly as specialists, understanding personalized information for a certain individual.
*   It proposes a novel one-shot learning framework that can learn individual characteristics from a single video.
*   The data augmentation pipeline and ReMoH mechanism are technically sound and contribute to the effectiveness of the model.
*   The experimental results demonstrate significant performance gains compared to state-of-the-art ViLLMs.
*   The dataset and code release will benefit the research community.

However, the reliance on synthetic data, limited evaluation of generalization, and framework complexity are weaknesses that slightly reduce the score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 0/10

### A Study into Investigating Temporal Robustness of LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17073v1)
- **Authors**: Jonas Wallat, Abdelrahman Abdallah, Adam Jatowt, Avishek Anand
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) encapsulate a surprising amount of factual world knowledge. However, their performance on temporal questions and historical knowledge is limited because they often cannot understand temporal scope and orientation or neglect the temporal aspect altogether. In this study, we aim to measure precisely how robust LLMs are for question answering based on their ability to process temporal information and perform tasks requiring temporal reasoning and temporal factual knowledge. Specifically, we design eight time-sensitive robustness tests for factual information to check the sensitivity of six popular LLMs in the zero-shot setting. Overall, we find LLMs lacking temporal robustness, especially to temporal reformulations and the use of different granularities of temporal references. We show how a selection of these eight tests can be used automatically to judge a model's temporal robustness for user questions on the fly. Finally, we apply the findings of this study to improve the temporal QA performance by up to 55 percent.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary of the paper, followed by a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance.

**Paper Summary**

The paper "A Study into Investigating Temporal Robustness of LLMs" investigates the ability of large language models (LLMs) to handle temporal information and perform tasks requiring temporal reasoning and factual knowledge. The authors design eight time-sensitive robustness tests to evaluate the sensitivity of several popular LLMs in a zero-shot setting. These tests focus on various aspects of temporal understanding, including temporal reversal, positioning, relativization, and the ability to handle different granularities of temporal references. The study reveals that LLMs often lack temporal robustness, particularly when dealing with temporal reformulations and varying temporal granularities. The authors demonstrate how a subset of these tests can be used to automatically assess a model's temporal robustness for user questions. Finally, the paper shows how the study's findings can improve temporal question-answering performance by applying guided reformulations.

**Critical Evaluation**

The paper tackles an important and under-explored area: the temporal robustness of LLMs. While LLMs have demonstrated impressive capabilities in many areas of natural language processing, their understanding and handling of temporal information is often inconsistent. This work provides a systematic and detailed investigation of this weakness.

**Strengths:**

*   **Systematic Evaluation Framework:** The authors define a clear set of tests to evaluate temporal robustness, covering various aspects like temporal reversal, time shifts, positioning, and relativization. This systematic approach allows for a more comprehensive understanding of the models' strengths and weaknesses.
*   **Practical Applications:** The paper goes beyond simply identifying limitations; it demonstrates how the developed tests can be used to improve the performance of LLMs in temporal QA tasks by guiding question reformulations. This offers a practical solution to mitigate the identified weaknesses.
*   **Automatic Test Suite:** The automatic robustness test suite is a valuable contribution, enabling the evaluation of LLMs' temporal understanding without the need for ground-truth answers. This is especially useful for real-world deployment scenarios.
*   **Comprehensive Benchmarking:** The experiments involve a reasonable selection of LLMs and well-established temporal QA datasets, providing a robust foundation for the findings.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of Temporal Reasoning:** The tests primarily focus on factual temporal knowledge and basic reasoning abilities. More complex temporal reasoning tasks, such as understanding event durations, simultaneous events, or temporal causality, are not extensively explored.
*   **Dataset Dependence:** While the datasets used are standard, the findings might be somewhat dataset-dependent. Different domains or types of temporal questions could reveal different strengths and weaknesses of the LLMs.
*   **Zero-Shot Setting:** The study is conducted in a zero-shot setting. Although this showcases the inherent capabilities of the LLMs, it does not explore how fine-tuning or other training techniques could improve temporal robustness.
*   **Limited Analysis of Error Types:** While the paper identifies performance drops under different transformations, a deeper qualitative analysis of the specific types of errors made by the LLMs could provide more actionable insights for improvement.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper's novelty lies in its systematic and comprehensive approach to evaluating the temporal robustness of LLMs. While other works have identified limitations in LLMs' temporal understanding, this study offers a well-defined framework, a suite of automatic tests, and practical methods for improving performance. The automatic test suite provides a way to test models without labeled data.

The significance of this work is in its potential to improve the reliability and trustworthiness of LLMs in tasks that require temporal reasoning. The identified weaknesses can inform the development of more robust models, and the proposed solutions can be directly applied to improve the performance of existing systems. As LLMs are increasingly used in applications involving historical data and temporal contexts, this research contributes to making them more reliable and accurate.

**Score and Justification**

I am assigning this paper a score of **7.5**. The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by systematically investigating and addressing a critical limitation of LLMs - their temporal robustness. The development of an automatic test suite and demonstrating its potential to improve QA performance makes this study practically useful. However, the scope of temporal reasoning covered by the tests could be broader, and the analysis of error types could be more in-depth. The dataset dependence and the zero-shot setting slightly limit the generalizability of the findings. These limitations reduce an otherwise very strong contribution.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Zero-Shot Styled Text Image Generation, but Make It Autoregressive
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17074v1)
- **Authors**: Vittorio Pippi, Fabio Quattrini, Silvia Cascianelli, Alessio Tonioni, Rita Cucchiara
- **Abstract**: Styled Handwritten Text Generation (HTG) has recently received attention from the computer vision and document analysis communities, which have developed several solutions, either GAN- or diffusion-based, that achieved promising results. Nonetheless, these strategies fail to generalize to novel styles and have technical constraints, particularly in terms of maximum output length and training efficiency. To overcome these limitations, in this work, we propose a novel framework for text image generation, dubbed Emuru. Our approach leverages a powerful text image representation model (a variational autoencoder) combined with an autoregressive Transformer. Our approach enables the generation of styled text images conditioned on textual content and style examples, such as specific fonts or handwriting styles. We train our model solely on a diverse, synthetic dataset of English text rendered in over 100,000 typewritten and calligraphy fonts, which gives it the capability to reproduce unseen styles (both fonts and users' handwriting) in zero-shot. To the best of our knowledge, Emuru is the first autoregressive model for HTG, and the first designed specifically for generalization to novel styles. Moreover, our model generates images without background artifacts, which are easier to use for downstream applications. Extensive evaluation on both typewritten and handwritten, any-length text image generation scenarios demonstrates the effectiveness of our approach.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Emuru," a novel autoregressive model for zero-shot styled text image generation. It addresses limitations of existing GAN- and diffusion-based approaches for handwritten text generation (HTG), such as poor generalization to new styles, constraints on output length, and background artifacts. Emuru uses a variational autoencoder (VAE) for text image representation, trained on a large, synthetic dataset of English text in various fonts, coupled with an autoregressive Transformer. This architecture allows Emuru to generate styled text images of arbitrary length, conditioned on style examples and text content, while avoiding background artifacts.  Extensive experiments on both typewritten and handwritten scenarios demonstrate Emuru's effectiveness and generalization abilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the architecture: an autoregressive Transformer combined with a VAE specifically designed for HTG. This is claimed to be the first autoregressive model for Offline HTG and is designed to generalize to novel styles. Another novel aspect is training solely on synthetic data, which aims to improve generalization. The combination of a strong VAE and Transformer-based generation with an explicit separation of style from background is a valuable contribution. The end-to-end autoregressive aspect, allowing generation of variable length text, is also a plus.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a relevant and challenging problem in document analysis and computer vision. Overcoming the limitations of existing HTG methods could have several practical applications, including synthetic data generation for other tasks, design, and assistive technologies. The zero-shot capability is also significant, as it reduces the need for task-specific training data. The synthetic data approach is a key step in making HTG practical.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The use of synthetic training data improves generalization.
    *   The VAE architecture effectively removes background artifacts.
    *   The autoregressive Transformer allows for generating text of arbitrary lengths.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate strong performance, particularly in generalization. The extensive experiments across multiple languages and style are a definite strength.
    *   The inclusion of a novel benchmark that will be publicly available will enable fair comparison.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the architecture is novel, the individual components (VAE and Transformer) are well-established. The real novelty comes from how they are combined and trained in this specific task.
    *   The use of only MSE as loss is somewhat limiting, and other loss functions might provide better performance.
    *   The lack of a mechanism to ensure text correctness is a possible limitation. In the results, there are mentions that the generated text does not maintain the correctness of text as compared to diffusion-based models.
    *   The qualitative comparisons and discussions are a bit limited. More in-depth analysis could have provided a more rigorous support for claims.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of the limitations and potential areas for future research.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the HTG field by offering a new approach that addresses several key limitations. The zero-shot capability and the avoidance of background artifacts are valuable contributions that could inspire further research. The availability of the code and weights will encourage adoption and further development.

*   **Justification of Score:**
    The paper presents a solid contribution to the HTG field, addressing several limitations of existing approaches. The combination of a VAE and an autoregressive Transformer is novel and offers several advantages, including improved generalization and arbitrary length text generation. However, the lack of ensuring that generated text maintains correctness is a critical concern that limits the model's practicality. The use of MSE and lack of ablative experiments might be considered shortcomings. The experimental results demonstrate promising performance, but further investigation of the model's limitations and potential areas for future research is needed. Therefore, a score of 7 is assigned, reflecting the paper's meaningful contribution and the potential for future development in this area.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Halton Scheduler For Masked Generative Image Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17076v1)
- **Authors**: Victor Besnier, Mickael Chen, David Hurych, Eduardo Valle, Matthieu Cord
- **Abstract**: Masked Generative Image Transformers (MaskGIT) have emerged as a scalable and efficient image generation framework, able to deliver high-quality visuals with low inference costs. However, MaskGIT's token unmasking scheduler, an essential component of the framework, has not received the attention it deserves. We analyze the sampling objective in MaskGIT, based on the mutual information between tokens, and elucidate its shortcomings. We then propose a new sampling strategy based on our Halton scheduler instead of the original Confidence scheduler. More precisely, our method selects the token's position according to a quasi-random, low-discrepancy Halton sequence. Intuitively, that method spreads the tokens spatially, progressively covering the image uniformly at each step. Our analysis shows that it allows reducing non-recoverable sampling errors, leading to simpler hyper-parameters tuning and better quality images. Our scheduler does not require retraining or noise injection and may serve as a simple drop-in replacement for the original sampling strategy. Evaluation of both class-to-image synthesis on ImageNet and text-to-image generation on the COCO dataset demonstrates that the Halton scheduler outperforms the Confidence scheduler quantitatively by reducing the FID and qualitatively by generating more diverse and more detailed images. Our code is at https://github.com/valeoai/Halton-MaskGIT.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel token unmasking scheduler, called the Halton scheduler, for Masked Generative Image Transformers (MaskGIT).  Instead of the commonly used Confidence scheduler (which unmasks the most "confident" tokens first), the Halton scheduler uses a quasi-random, low-discrepancy Halton sequence to select token positions. The authors argue that this spatially spreads the tokens more uniformly, reducing the correlation between simultaneously sampled tokens and maximizing information gain at each step, leading to more diverse and detailed images. The paper presents a mutual information analysis to justify the approach and demonstrates improved performance compared to the Confidence scheduler on both class-conditional ImageNet and text-to-image COCO datasets in terms of FID, Inception Score, Precision, and Recall. Importantly, the proposed scheduler is a drop-in replacement, requiring no retraining or noise injection.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using a low-discrepancy sequence like the Halton sequence for token selection in MaskGIT *is* a novel contribution.  While MaskGIT has been extended and modified in several works, the unmasking schedule component has received less attention. The theoretical justification using mutual information is also a valuable contribution to understanding MaskGIT's operation. The closest competing method appears to be "QLDS," mentioned in the related works section. However, it's intended for compression rather than improving generative capability, which differentiates the purpose and application of this work.
*   **Significance:** The improvements reported by the authors are significant.  Lowering the FID score without requiring retraining or noise injection is a valuable practical benefit. The drop-in replacement nature of the Halton scheduler makes it immediately usable with existing MaskGIT models. The qualitative improvements, specifically the increased diversity and detail in generated images, are also noteworthy. The analysis of the shortcomings of the Confidence scheduler and the proposal of the Halton sequence as a solution is well-justified and explained.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Strong theoretical justification based on mutual information.
    *   Effective and easy-to-implement solution (Halton scheduler).
    *   Consistent improvement over the Confidence scheduler across different datasets and tasks.
    *   Drop-in replacement, requires no retraining.
    *   Provides thorough ablation studies validating the impact of varying number of steps.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on Euclidean distance in the token space as a proxy for mutual information, although justified empirically, could be a limitation. There might be better ways to estimate the dependency.
    *   The paper acknowledges limitations in handling long-range dependencies, which could be explored further. Addressing this may increase the novelty and impact of the work.
    *   Failure cases are mentioned, but not investigated in depth. Better understanding and addressing specific failure modes could lead to further improvements.
    *   The comparison with other approaches is somewhat limited. The comparison table mainly shows results on ImageNet, so it would be nice to have similar comparisons with other recent text-to-image generation methods.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to become a standard component in future MaskGIT applications. The Halton scheduler can be easily adopted by researchers and practitioners working with MaskGIT. The conceptual analysis of the unmasking scheduler can also guide future work. The reported improved results and ability to work as a simple drop-in replacement strategy contribute to a notable impact.
* The paper could also influence new schedulers with better long-range dependency.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:** The paper offers a novel and practical improvement to MaskGIT that is well-justified both theoretically and empirically. The fact that it's a drop-in replacement adds significant value. While the reliance on Euclidean distance in feature space could be improved and long-range dependencies are a limitation, the overall contribution is substantial. This score reflects the solid methodological contribution and potential to influence future adoption in MaskGIT workflows, tempered by certain limitations, particularly in regards to addressing a full set of failure scenarios, or comparisons to text-to-image generation models.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Deterministic AI Agent Personality Expression through Standard Psychological Diagnostics
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17085v1)
- **Authors**: J. M. Diederik Kruijssen, Nicholas Emmons
- **Abstract**: Artificial intelligence (AI) systems powered by large language models have become increasingly prevalent in modern society, enabling a wide range of applications through natural language interaction. As AI agents proliferate in our daily lives, their generic and uniform expressiveness presents a significant limitation to their appeal and adoption. Personality expression represents a key prerequisite for creating more human-like and distinctive AI systems. We show that AI models can express deterministic and consistent personalities when instructed using established psychological frameworks, with varying degrees of accuracy depending on model capabilities. We find that more advanced models like GPT-4o and o1 demonstrate the highest accuracy in expressing specified personalities across both Big Five and Myers-Briggs assessments, and further analysis suggests that personality expression emerges from a combination of intelligence and reasoning capabilities. Our results reveal that personality expression operates through holistic reasoning rather than question-by-question optimization, with response-scale metrics showing higher variance than test-scale metrics. Furthermore, we find that model fine-tuning affects communication style independently of personality expression accuracy. These findings establish a foundation for creating AI agents with diverse and consistent personalities, which could significantly enhance human-AI interaction across applications from education to healthcare, while additionally enabling a broader range of more unique AI agents. The ability to quantitatively assess and implement personality expression in AI systems opens new avenues for research into more relatable, trustworthy, and ethically designed AI.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the limitation of generic expressiveness in AI agents powered by Large Language Models (LLMs).  It proposes a framework to create AI agents with diverse and consistent personalities using established psychological diagnostics, namely the Big Five Personality Test and the Myers-Briggs Type Indicator (MBTI). The authors show that AI models can express deterministic personalities when instructed using these frameworks, with the accuracy depending on model capabilities. More advanced models like GPT-4o and o1 demonstrate the highest accuracy. Analysis reveals that personality expression involves holistic reasoning rather than question-by-question optimization. The study also explores the influence of fine-tuning on communication style and reasoning context on personality expression.  The authors suggest that this ability to quantitatively assess and implement personality expression has the potential to improve human-AI interaction and promote ethically designed AI.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of injecting personality into AI agents isn't entirely new (previous work has explored similar concepts). However, the **rigorous, quantitative approach** using established psychological frameworks is a key differentiator.  The systematic exploration of how *different AI models* perform at expressing these personalities, and the dissection of the underlying reasoning processes (holistic vs. question-by-question), contribute to the novelty. Also, the paper clearly states previous initial steps have been made to direct and quantify AI agent personality expression (e.g. Cheng et al., 2024; Park et al., 2024; Ren & Xu, 2025) and then defines their value and difference.
*   **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing a key challenge in the proliferation of AI agents: their blandness.  Developing agents with distinct personalities is crucial for improving user engagement, trust, and overall adoption.  The paper's framework could significantly impact fields like education (personalized learning), healthcare (more relatable AI assistants), and customer service (engaging and empathetic chatbots).
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Systematic Approach:** The use of established psychological frameworks provides a structured and quantifiable way to define and assess personality in AI agents.
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The paper examines various factors affecting personality expression, including model type, fine-tuning, and reasoning context.
    *   **Dissection of Reasoning Process:** Analysis using response scale metrics to measure variance, this process provided insights into how AI express personality, and that it operates in an average way.
    *   **Real-World Applicability:** The framework is directly applicable to AI agent development and could have a wide range of practical applications.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Personality Tests:** Focus on only two personality tests. While common, exploring a wider range of psychological assessments could strengthen the generalization of the findings.
    *   **Contextual Limitations:** The tests were performed within a limited context (text-based questionnaires). The generalizability of the framework to more complex and dynamic situations (e.g., real-time interactions, multimodal communication) needs further investigation.
    *   **Openness Accuracy:** Inaccuracy of the models when it came to matching agents with a corresponding personality test and then having the same result regarding openness. The limited diversity and narrow range of the model was most likely the factor.
    *   **Ethical Considerations:** While the paper briefly touches on ethical considerations, a more in-depth discussion of the potential risks of using AI personalities to manipulate users would be valuable.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper provides a solid foundation for future research in this area. It opens new avenues for exploring the relationship between personality, AI, and human interaction. The results can guide the development of more relatable, trustworthy, and ethically designed AI systems. It could also spur further exploration into cognitive biases in AI.
*   **Rigorous Rationale** The paper is scientifically rigorous, the methodology is well-defined, and the results are presented clearly and supported by evidence. The thorough analysis of the different models and factors affecting personality expression further strengthens the paper's credibility.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:** The paper showcases notable novelty by providing a structured approach to injecting personality into AI agents using proven psychological frameworks.  While the *idea* of AI personalities isn't groundbreaking, the quantitative framework and comprehensive analysis of influencing factors set it apart.  The study's limitations (limited tests, contextual constraints, limited diversity) prevent a higher score, but its potential impact on improving human-AI interaction and ethically designing AI is significant.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### R2LDM: An Efficient 4D Radar Super-Resolution Framework Leveraging Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17097v1)
- **Authors**: Boyuan Zheng, Shouyi Lu, Renbo Huang, Minqing Huang, Fan Lu, Wei Tian, Guirong Zhuo, Lu Xiong
- **Abstract**: We introduce R2LDM, an innovative approach for generating dense and accurate 4D radar point clouds, guided by corresponding LiDAR point clouds. Instead of utilizing range images or bird's eye view (BEV) images, we represent both LiDAR and 4D radar point clouds using voxel features, which more effectively capture 3D shape information. Subsequently, we propose the Latent Voxel Diffusion Model (LVDM), which performs the diffusion process in the latent space. Additionally, a novel Latent Point Cloud Reconstruction (LPCR) module is utilized to reconstruct point clouds from high-dimensional latent voxel features. As a result, R2LDM effectively generates LiDAR-like point clouds from paired raw radar data. We evaluate our approach on two different datasets, and the experimental results demonstrate that our model achieves 6- to 10-fold densification of radar point clouds, outperforming state-of-the-art baselines in 4D radar point cloud super-resolution. Furthermore, the enhanced radar point clouds generated by our method significantly improve downstream tasks, achieving up to 31.7% improvement in point cloud registration recall rate and 24.9% improvement in object detection accuracy.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces R2LDM, a novel framework for 4D radar point cloud super-resolution.  It addresses the challenge of sparse and noisy radar data by leveraging a latent voxel diffusion model (LVDM) to fuse radar and LiDAR information within a shared latent space. This latent representation captures 3D structural information more effectively than traditional range image or BEV encodings. The framework also incorporates a latent point cloud reconstruction (LPCR) module to generate dense, LiDAR-like point clouds from the latent features.  A two-stage training strategy is employed. The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on two datasets, showing improved point cloud registration and object detection accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Problem Significance:** Addressing the sparsity and noise inherent in 4D radar data is a crucial challenge for enabling reliable all-weather autonomous driving.
    *   **Novel Approach:** The use of a latent voxel diffusion model to combine radar and LiDAR information is a novel approach. Representing point clouds as latent voxel features, and then doing diffusion in this space to generate LiDAR-like point clouds from sparse Radar data.  This avoids limitations of direct point cloud processing and range image encodings.
    *   **LPCR Module:** The latent point cloud reconstruction module is well-designed to reconstruct point clouds from latent features.
    *   **Two-Stage Training:** This strategy is key to handling data with varying densities, resulting in robust reconstruction.
    *   **Demonstrated Performance:** The paper provides experimental results on multiple datasets that outperform existing state-of-the-art methods.
    *   **Downstream Task Improvement:** The generated point clouds improve downstream tasks like registration and object detection.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** Although the paper mentions resource conservation during training, the inference time of a diffusion model for 3D point clouds can be significant. The computational cost might restrict its practical use in real-time applications. A discussion on the memory and time complexity of the model is lacking.
    *   **Dataset Dependency:** Performance may be highly dependent on the quality and diversity of the training data. Results from more diverse, real-world datasets would strengthen the paper's claims.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:** Diffusion models can be sensitive to hyperparameter tuning. The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the impact of various hyperparameters on performance.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provided were excellent for their type and detailed analyses.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   **Novelty:** The core idea of using a latent voxel diffusion model for radar super-resolution, guided by LiDAR data, is a significant contribution. The LPCR module for reconstructing point clouds from latent features is also novel.
    *   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important problem in autonomous driving and presents a promising solution. If the method can be made more computationally efficient, it has the potential to improve the reliability of autonomous vehicles in challenging weather conditions.

*   **Influence on the Field:**

    *   The paper has the potential to influence research in the following areas:

        *   4D radar processing and perception
        *   Sensor fusion techniques
        *   Generative models for point cloud data
        *   Autonomous driving in adverse weather

**Score:** 8

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to an important problem. The results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance and improved downstream task accuracy. The primary weakness is the potential computational cost of the method and lack of such analysis in the paper. The paper has the potential to influence future research in radar processing, sensor fusion, and autonomous driving. The overall approach and achieved results are significant enough to warrant a high score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Large Language Model Compression via the Nested Activation-Aware Decomposition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17101v1)
- **Authors**: Jun Lu, Tianyi Xu, Bill Ding, David Li, Yu Kang
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we tackle the critical challenge of compressing large language models (LLMs) to facilitate their practical deployment and broader adoption. We introduce a novel post-training compression paradigm that focuses on low-rank decomposition of LLM weights. Our analysis identifies two main challenges in this task: the variability in LLM activation distributions and handling unseen activations from different datasets and models. To address these challenges, we propose a nested activation-aware framework (NSVD) for LLMs, a training-free approach designed to enhance the accuracy of low-rank decompositions by managing activation outliers through transforming the weight matrix based on activation distribution and the original weight matrix. This method allows for the absorption of outliers into the transformed weight matrix, improving decomposition accuracy. Our comprehensive evaluation across eight datasets and six models from three distinct LLM families demonstrates the superiority of NSVD over current state-of-the-art methods, especially at medium to large compression ratios or in multilingual and multitask settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper introduces a novel post-training compression method for large language models (LLMs) called Nested Activation-Aware Decomposition (NSVD). NSVD focuses on low-rank decomposition of LLM weights and addresses challenges related to the variability in LLM activation distributions and handling unseen activations from diverse datasets and models. The proposed method is training-free and designed to improve the accuracy of low-rank decompositions by managing activation outliers. It transforms the weight matrix based on the activation distribution and the original weight matrix, allowing for the absorption of outliers. The paper presents comprehensive evaluations on several datasets and LLMs, demonstrating NSVD's superiority over existing state-of-the-art methods, especially at medium to high compression ratios or in multilingual/multitask settings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the proposed NSVD framework, which explicitly addresses the limitations of existing low-rank approximation methods for LLM compression. The two key components, "truncation-aware data whitening" and "two-way decomposition," appear to be novel and tailored to the specific challenges presented by LLM compression.  While prior work has considered activation awareness, NSVD's approach to incorporating it into the low-rank decomposition process seems more sophisticated than directly scaling the weight matrix using Fisher information or Cholesky decompositions. The nesting of decompositions provides a finer control on compression.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from the increasing importance of LLM compression for practical deployment and broader adoption.  If NSVD can consistently achieve better compression ratios with minimal accuracy degradation, it can have a real impact. The experiments include a reasonable range of models, datasets and compression ratios, lending credibility to the results. However, the zero-shot setting means the models are sensitive to data.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem statement:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of compressing LLMs and the limitations of existing approaches.
    *   **Well-motivated approach:** The NSVD framework is well-motivated and addresses the identified challenges.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The experiments are thorough, covering multiple datasets, models, and compression ratios. The comparisons to strong baselines like ASVD are essential.
    *   **Strong results:** The experimental results demonstrate that NSVD outperforms existing methods, particularly at medium to high compression ratios.
    *   **Theoretical justification:** The paper provides theoretical justification (Theorems 2, 3, and 4) for their approach, linking it to SVD and related concepts.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The nested approach adds some complexity compared to standard SVD. While the paper claims the computational complexity is same as ASVD, the approach is not yet widely adopted and tested.
    *   **Tuning `k1` and `k2`:** The performance is sensitive to selecting `k1` (and therefore `k2`), which need to be tuned based on different tasks and datasets. It can be potentially improved with automated tuning.
    *   **Lack of code/implementation:** Code availability (or lack thereof) hinders reproducibility and wider adoption.  If the method is truly as effective as claimed, open-sourcing the code would significantly increase its impact.
    *   **Zero-Shot setting limitations:** The model sensitivity can be addressed with fine-tuning. However, fine-tuning would increase model complexity.
*   **Potential Influence:** If the claims hold up with further validation and the code is made available, NSVD could become a popular technique for LLM compression, enabling more efficient deployment on resource-constrained devices. The nested decomposition idea could also inspire other compression techniques.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the novelty of the NSVD framework, the well-motivated approach, and the thorough experimental evaluation, the paper demonstrates a significant contribution to LLM compression. However, the lack of code availability, the complexity of tuning, the sensitivity to data, and the fact that this research has not yet stood the test of time in the community brings the rating down.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Modifying Large Language Model Post-Training for Diverse Creative Writing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17126v1)
- **Authors**: John Joon Young Chung, Vishakh Padmakumar, Melissa Roemmele, Yuqian Sun, Max Kreminski
- **Abstract**: As creative writing tasks do not have singular correct answers, large language models (LLMs) trained to perform these tasks should be able to generate diverse valid outputs. However, LLM post-training often focuses on improving generation quality but neglects to facilitate output diversity. Hence, in creative writing generation, we investigate post-training approaches to promote both output diversity and quality. Our core idea is to include deviation -- the degree of difference between a training sample and all other samples with the same prompt -- in the training objective to facilitate learning from rare high-quality instances. By adopting our approach to direct preference optimization (DPO) and odds ratio preference optimization (ORPO), we demonstrate that we can promote the output diversity of trained models while minimally decreasing quality. Our best model with 8B parameters could achieve on-par diversity as a human-created dataset while having output quality similar to the best instruction-tuned models we examined, GPT-4o and DeepSeek-R1. We further validate our approaches with a human evaluation, an ablation, and a comparison to an existing diversification approach, DivPO.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of low output diversity in large language models (LLMs) when applied to creative writing tasks. While post-training techniques like DPO and ORPO improve quality, they often reduce diversity. The authors propose a modification to these methods, called "Diversified DPO" (DDPO) and "Diversified ORPO" (DORPO), which incorporate a "deviation" score—measuring how different a training sample is from others with the same prompt—into the training objective.  The paper shows that DDPO and DORPO can improve diversity with minimal impact on quality, even achieving diversity comparable to human-written text while maintaining quality comparable to instruction-tuned models. They validate this through automatic metrics, human evaluation, ablation studies, and comparison to another diversification technique (DivPO).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The central idea of incorporating a "deviation" score directly into the DPO/ORPO objective is the paper's main contribution. It's a straightforward but effective way to encourage the LLM to learn from less frequent, yet still high-quality, responses. This isn't a completely new concept (diversity-aware training is explored elsewhere), but its specific application and implementation within the DPO/ORPO framework, tailored for creative writing, appears novel. The introduction of semantic and style deviations in the loss functions is also a good touch. The comparison with DivPO is useful to contextualize their contributions.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the practical problem it tackles. Low diversity in creative writing LLMs limits their usefulness in scenarios where varied and original ideas are desired. Improving diversity without sacrificing quality is a vital step towards more useful and versatile creative AI tools. The paper's findings suggest that carefully modifying post-training objectives can effectively address this challenge. The experimental results are promising, demonstrating that the proposed approach can achieve a good balance between quality and diversity. The human evaluation also strengthens the argument that DDPO and DORPO models are favored in terms of creativity. Ablation tests give insight into the dependency of a sufficient number of instances per prompt.
    The limitations are adequately discussed, particularly the dependency on having enough instances per prompt. The observation and analysis of why and how their approach helps with surface-level diversity are valuable, and the insights into why deepseek-R1 behaves differently are good.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined approach (DDPO/DORPO with deviation score).
    *   Comprehensive experiments: automatic metrics, human evaluation, ablation, comparison to DivPO.
    *   Detailed analysis of the results and limitations.
    *   Easy-to-understand writing style.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The "deviation" score is a relatively simple metric, and the results are somewhat dependent on the performance of the embedding models used to calculate it. While effective, more sophisticated measures of content difference could potentially yield even better results.
    *   The experimental evaluation is limited to a specific creative writing dataset. While the results are promising, it's unclear how well the approach would generalize to other datasets or creative tasks.
    *   While human evaluation exists, the set is small, and the human agreement may not be so high.
    *   The lack of online training results also limits the scope of the paper.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research on post-training LLMs, particularly for creative applications. It highlights the importance of considering diversity as a specific optimization goal, not just focusing on quality. It provides a concrete method for achieving this and demonstrates its effectiveness. The paper could also inspire researchers to explore more sophisticated ways of measuring and promoting diversity in LLM outputs.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a clear, well-executed, and valuable contribution to the field. It introduces a relatively simple but effective modification to existing LLM post-training techniques to improve diversity in creative writing generation. The experiments are thorough and support the paper's claims. However, the simplicity of the "deviation" score, the limited scope of the experimental evaluation, and the lack of online training results prevent a higher score. While the paper has a valuable contribution, it's not necessarily a groundbreaking innovation that will fundamentally change the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### HiFi-Stream: Streaming Speech Enhancement with Generative Adversarial Networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17141v1)
- **Authors**: Ekaterina Dmitrieva, Maksim Kaledin
- **Abstract**: Speech Enhancement techniques have become core technologies in mobile devices and voice software simplifying downstream speech tasks. Still, modern Deep Learning (DL) solutions often require high amount of computational resources what makes their usage on low-resource devices challenging. We present HiFi-Stream, an optimized version of recently published HiFi++ model. Our experiments demonstrate that HiFiStream saves most of the qualities of the original model despite its size and computational complexity: the lightest version has only around 490k parameters which is 3.5x reduction in comparison to the original HiFi++ making it one of the smallest and fastest models available. The model is evaluated in streaming setting where it demonstrates its superior performance in comparison to modern baselines.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "HiFi-Stream: Streaming Speech Enhancement with Generative Adversarial Networks."

**Summary:**

The paper presents HiFi-Stream, an optimized version of the HiFi++ model for speech enhancement (SE). The core goal is to reduce the computational complexity and parameter size of HiFi++ to make it suitable for resource-constrained devices. The authors introduce structural and technical modifications including removing spectral U-Net, improved HiFi block based on 2D convolutions and optimized channel number, and introducing Feature Map Scaling (FMS) based mask net. Experiments demonstrate that HiFi-Stream achieves a significant reduction in parameters and MACs while maintaining comparable performance to HiFi++ in both offline and streaming scenarios. The model is evaluated on VCTK dataset and compared with other GAN-based lightweight solutions in terms of perceptual evaluation metrics and objective evaluation metrics like SDR.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its optimization strategy.  While HiFi++ already aimed at reducing model complexity compared to previous GAN-based SE methods, HiFi-Stream takes this further. The specific techniques like replacing Spectral U-Net and redesign of HiFi-block using 2D convolutions and FMS in the MaskNet block, and the experiments surrounding their impact on both model size and performance represent a valuable incremental contribution. However, the underlying architecture remains heavily based on HiFi++, so the novelty is not groundbreaking.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is directly related to the growing need for deploying SE models on low-resource devices, especially in mobile and wearable applications. Reducing computational complexity without significant performance degradation is a critical challenge. HiFi-Stream makes tangible progress in this direction, offering a more practical alternative to larger, more computationally intensive models. The streaming evaluation, though preliminary, is also significant as it focuses on a real-world deployment scenario. Demonstrating that the model retains much of its quality in a streaming setting (i.e., chunk-by-chunk processing) is a crucial step. The experiments shows the model maintains robustness in performance even when input is limited.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Concrete Optimization:** The paper presents specific, well-defined modifications to HiFi++. The descriptions of how each change affects model size, computational complexity, and performance are valuable.
    *   **Thorough Evaluation:** The evaluation includes a range of metrics (perceptual, objective) and different scenarios (offline, streaming). This provides a relatively comprehensive assessment of the model's capabilities and limitations.
    *   **Comparison to Baselines:** Comparing HiFi-Stream to existing low-parameter SE models provides context and highlights the trade-offs achieved by the proposed approach.
    *   **Github repository link:** The reproduction of the model can easily be done with the link.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Improvement:** As noted above, the novelty is largely incremental. The fundamental architecture is still HiFi++, and the optimization techniques are not necessarily revolutionary.
    *   **Limited Streaming Evaluation:** The streaming evaluation, while valuable, is relatively simple. It merely concatenates the result of audio chunks without further training. More extensive evaluation of streaming scenarios, including online training or adaptation, would be beneficial.
    *   **Dataset:** Only single dataset VCTK is used.
    *   **Ablation study:** Only very high level ablation study is done, more detail could be more valuable.

*   **Potential Influence:** HiFi-Stream could influence future research by demonstrating that significant model reduction is possible in GAN-based SE without sacrificing too much quality. It also highlights the importance of considering computational complexity alongside perceptual metrics when designing SE models for real-world applications. The model is robust to context limitations with the help of FMS.

**Justification for Score:**

HiFi-Stream represents a valuable contribution to the field of speech enhancement, but the incremental nature of its novelty prevents it from achieving a higher score. The optimizations are well-executed and the evaluation is thorough, demonstrating clear improvements in computational efficiency. However, the work builds heavily on the HiFi++ architecture and lacks more in-depth investigation of streaming adaptation strategies. While the results are promising, more extensive real-world streaming evaluations would solidify the model's practical applicability.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 6/10

### D2C: Unlocking the Potential of Continuous Autoregressive Image Generation with Discrete Tokens
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17155v1)
- **Authors**: Panpan Wang, Liqiang Niu, Fandong Meng, Jinan Xu, Yufeng Chen, Jie Zhou
- **Abstract**: In the domain of image generation, latent-based generative models occupy a dominant status; however, these models rely heavily on image tokenizer. To meet modeling requirements, autoregressive models possessing the characteristics of scalability and flexibility embrace a discrete-valued tokenizer, but face the challenge of poor image generation quality. In contrast, diffusion models take advantage of the continuous-valued tokenizer to achieve better generation quality but are subject to low efficiency and complexity. The existing hybrid models are mainly to compensate for information loss and simplify the diffusion learning process. The potential of merging discrete-valued and continuous-valued tokens in the field of image generation has not yet been explored. In this paper, we propose D2C, a novel two-stage method to enhance model generation capacity. In the first stage, the discrete-valued tokens representing coarse-grained image features are sampled by employing a small discrete-valued generator. Then in the second stage, the continuous-valued tokens representing fine-grained image features are learned conditioned on the discrete token sequence. In addition, we design two kinds of fusion modules for seamless interaction. On the ImageNet-256 benchmark, extensive experiment results validate that our model achieves superior performance compared with several continuous-valued and discrete-valued generative models on the class-conditional image generation tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces D2C, a novel two-stage hybrid autoregressive framework for image generation.  D2C aims to improve image generation quality by combining discrete-valued and continuous-valued tokens. In the first stage, a small discrete-valued autoregressive model generates a sequence of discrete tokens representing coarse-grained image features.  In the second stage, a hybrid autoregressive model learns to generate continuous-valued tokens (representing fine-grained image features) conditioned on the discrete token sequence and the class token.  The authors design two types of fusion modules (cross-attention and Q-Former) to facilitate interaction between the discrete and continuous token streams. They perform experiments on the ImageNet-256 benchmark, demonstrating superior performance compared to existing continuous-valued and discrete-valued generative models on class-conditional image generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of combining discrete and continuous tokens in a *progressive, two-stage autoregressive* fashion is a worthwhile exploration, although the concept of using discrete tokens to guide a continuous process is not entirely new, as referenced by the authors in the discussion on DisCo-Diff and HART. However, their approach stands out due to its specific architecture: specifically, different *pre-trained* tokenizers producing coarse and fine grained information, which are used sequentially. The authors clearly differentiate their approach from other existing models using hybrid token strategies. The design of two kinds of fusion modules also contributes a novel interaction mechanism.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses limitations of both discrete and continuous generative models and tries to remedy them by merging their strengths, specifically the scalability and flexibility of autoregressive discrete token models and the high quality of continuous generative models (diffusion models). Demonstrating improved image generation quality is inherently significant. The reported performance gains on ImageNet-256 over existing models provide empirical evidence of the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The speed improvements over MAR are also a meaningful contribution.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The proposed D2C framework appears well-designed and logically sound.
    *   The experimental results are strong and the authors compare against a comprehensive set of baselines.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the contributions of different components (fusion modules, number of query tokens).
    *   The visualizations illustrate the qualitative improvements achieved by the D2C model.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The architecture relies on pre-trained image tokenizers (VQGAN and VAE). While this simplifies training, it ties the performance to the quality of these tokenizers. The authors address this in the "Discussions" section, but it warrants more explicit discussion in relation to limitations.
    *   The paper focuses primarily on class-conditional image generation. While this is a common benchmark, the generalizability of D2C to other image generation tasks (e.g., text-to-image) is not explored.
    *   Some aspects of the implementation (e.g., specific hyperparameter settings for training the D2C-B and D2C-L models) could be described in more detail.

*   **Potential Impact:** The D2C framework could influence future research on hybrid generative models.  The fusion modules could be adapted to other architectures. The idea of using discrete tokens to guide continuous-valued token generation could inspire new approaches in other domains.

* **Justification of score:**
While the idea of combining discrete and continuous token approaches isn't completely new, the specific architecture, which is a *two-stage, progressive autoregressive model with independently trained discrete and continuous tokenizers*, and the implementation of fusion modules do provide some incremental novelty.  The results on ImageNet-256 clearly demonstrate improved performance over several strong baselines, justifying the potential for significant influence in the field. The ablation studies add further credibility. It's held back by reliance on pre-trained tokenizers and the limited exploration of tasks beyond class-conditional image generation. Also, while MAR speed is significantly outpaced, other methods from the comparison table are much faster, therefore its general contribution would be diminished if inference speed were the key objective.
